Well, when I'm saying the numbers, it's just... I'm just trying to keep track, you know? Like, the numbers are important. Table four, table seven, table nine... Nine is just... it's a good number. No, Almond Joy. Almonds are okay, I guess, but they get stuck in your teeth.

Schizoaffective disorder (F25.x) is less likely at this point due to insufficient evidence of mood symptoms. Schizophrenia (F20.x) remains possible but less supported by current information. The patient does not yet show clear evidence of delusions or hallucinations necessary for F20.0 or F20.6, and no clear catatonic features to support F20.2. Organic causes are not indicated, so F06.1 (psychotic disorder due to another medical condition) is not yet in focus.",

My hair gets so frizzy when it's humid. I tried this new conditioner, but it smells like coconut, and I really hate coconut. Almonds are okay, I guess, but they get stuck in your teeth. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? The truck? I just don't know why he has to park in spot nine. There are plenty of other spots.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt something that others around you don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes... like when I'm doing the closing shift, or even just at home in my own kitchen. I'll hear this woman's voice. She says my name, or she tells me I'm doing the register count wrong. It's so clear, like she's right next to me, and I'll actually answer her out loud before I realize no one else is there. \n\nMy kitchen at home has these awful yellow cabinets. I don't know who picked them out, probably the landlord. Landlords never have good taste. He came over last month to fix the sink, and he tracked mud all over my rug. It's a nice rug, too, I got it at Target on clearance. Target always has the best clearance section if you go on a Tuesday morning. I bought a really nice throw pillow there once, but my cat threw up on it. Cats are so sensitive, you know? You change their food one time, and suddenly they're sick everywhere. I used to buy the chicken flavor, but then they discontinued it, which is ridiculous because chicken is, like, the most basic flavor. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice... I just hear her sometimes, and I'm the only one who does.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't real or have a hard time understanding, like a situation where you know something is happening but other people deny it?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. No, I don't think so. Like, things I read or see don't have secret messages for me. I read a lot of menus, obviously, working at the restaurant. We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. But this guy was squinting at the menu for like ten minutes and finally asked me what the soup of the day was because the cursive made minestrone look like a bunch of squiggles. I don't even like minestrone. Too many beans. Beans always upset my stomach, you know? I prefer a good potato soup, especially when it's cold out. It's supposed to rain tomorrow, actually. I need to remember to bring my umbrella. I always leave it in the backseat of my car.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, it does get really confusing. Like, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up right in the middle of service. Or I'll do things totally out of order. Like the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.

evening shift lead responsibilities.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The clearer voices and firm beliefs about coworkers and customers targeting me have been happening for about four months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind that aren’t yours, or like things happening around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like thoughts are put in my head. My thoughts are my own, you know? Like, I have a lot of them, especially when it's busy at work. But they're mine. I did think about getting my hair cut short, though. Like a bob? A lot of the girls at work are doing it. Sarah just got hers cut, and it looks really cute, but she has that straight hair. Like, things I read or see don't have secret messages for me. I read a lot of menus, obviously, working at the restaurant. We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. I don't even like minestrone. Too many beans. Beans always upset my stomach, you know? I prefer a good potato soup, especially when it's cold out. It's supposed to rain tomorrow, actually. Does it feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping around so much that you can't focus on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it does get really confusing. Like, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up right in the middle of service. Or I'll do things totally out of order. Like the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I've never felt like that. My thoughts stay in my head. I mean, nobody can hear what I'm thinking. That would be crazy, right? If people could hear my thoughts, I'd probably get fired because I think some pretty mean things about the customers sometimes. Like this one lady who always asks for hot water with lemon instead of tea, but then she brings her own tea bags in her purse. It's so cheap. And she always leaves a terrible tip. Usually just a handful of change, like nickels and dimes. Who pays with nickels anymore? They're so heavy in your apron pocket. My apron gets so heavy by the end of the shift, it actually pulls on my neck. I need to get one of those aprons that ties around the waist instead of going over the neck. My manager says we have to wear the neck ones because it has the company logo right on the chest, but it's really bad for your posture. I read an article online about how bad posture can cause tension headaches, and I get headaches all the time. I usually just take ibuprofen, but sometimes it doesn't work. 

But no, my thoughts are definitely private. No one is taking them or hearing them.

We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. But this guy was squinting at the menu for like ten minutes and finally asked me what the soup of the day was because the cursive made minestrone look like a bunch of squiggles. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't think so. I mean, I speak perfectly fine. People understand me. You have to be able to talk clearly to be a waitress, you know? Communication is like, the most important part of the job. If you don't make sense, you mess up the orders. And people get really mad if you mess up their food. Especially with allergies. You have to be so specific. Gluten allergies are huge right now. It seems like everyone suddenly can't eat gluten. We have this gluten-free bun option for our burgers, but honestly, it crumbles the second you pick it up. It's basically like eating sand. I don't know why people pay two dollars extra for it. I love bread too much to ever give it up. Sourdough is my absolute favorite. I actually tried making a sourdough starter a couple of years ago, but I kept forgetting to feed it and it grew this gross, fuzzy green mold. It smelled just like old beer. My apartment kind of smells weird anyway, though, because the air vents are connected to the guy downstairs, and he cooks with so much garlic. Garlic is great, but not when you're smelling it at eight in the morning. It really gets trapped in the curtains. I bought these heavy blackout curtains because the streetlight outside my bedroom window is blinding. They're this really dark plum color... 

But yeah, nobody has ever told me I don't make sense. My sentences are fine.

It makes this awful crackling noise every time there's a heavy bass line. I've asked Mr. Henderson, the owner, to fix it like a hundred times, but he never wants to spend money on anything. He won't even replace the salt shakers. The glass on half of them is chipped, and last week the lid just completely fell off one while a guy was using it, and a whole mountain of salt went right onto his fries. He was so mad. But honestly, our fries aren't even that good unless they're fresh out of the fryer. If they sit under the heat lamp for more than five minutes, they get all soggy. I like mine super crispy, and I dip them in mayonnaise instead of ketchup. I know some people think that's weird, but I heard they do it in Europe. I'd love to go to Europe someday. I actually took a French class when I was in college for a little bit, but I had to drop it because the textbook was insanely expensive. It was like two hundred dollars just for a paperback... but yeah, no, my thoughts are just my own. Nobody is taking them.

No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head. That sounds like something out of a sci-fi movie, you know? Like those movies where aliens take over people's brains. I watched this one movie late last night because I couldn't sleep. My upstairs neighbor got a new puppy and it was whining and scratching at the floor for hours. Although half the time it was completely covered in dog drool by the time my dad got it. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... 

But no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.

========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{
  "candidates": ["F23.2", "F21", "F20.8"],
  "note": "The patient has consistently denied thought insertion, thought withdrawal, and thought broadcasting, which makes F23.1 and F22 less likely. The presence of persistent, odd beliefs (e.g., about the coworkers plotting against her, voices that comment on her actions), and disorganized behavior (e.g., freezing up at work, repetitive speech) supports a psychotic disorder. F23.2 (acute polymorphic psychotic disorder without symptoms of schizophrenia) remains likely due to the acute onset and polymorphic symptoms including perceptual disturbances and odd beliefs. F21 (schizotypal disorder) remains due to the presence of eccentric behavior and odd beliefs, though it is typically non-psychotic and long-standing, which is less clear in this case. F20.8 (other schizophrenia) remains as a broad category for atypical presentations of schizophrenia that may not fit neatly into other subtypes. Further evaluation of duration and functional impairment would help clarify between these.",
  "is_final": false
}

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't feel like they jump around. I mean, maybe I talk a lot, but everything connects in my head. It's just like how conversations work, right? You start talking about one thing, and it reminds you of something else. Like, the other day at the diner, a customer asked for extra pickles, which reminded me of my aunt's homemade pickles. She used to can them every summer in these huge mason jars. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. He was always so serious, even when we were kids. He's an accountant now, which totally makes sense. He loves numbers. I hate math, honestly. Trying to figure out the tip at the end of a shift when the register acts up is the worst part of my night... but yeah, my thoughts don't just jump without a reason. There's always a reason they go where they go.

Nine is my lucky number, actually. I always try to park in spot number nine at my apartment complex, but this guy with a red truck keeps taking it. It's a nice rug, too, I got it at Target on clearance. Target always has the best clearance section if you go on a Tuesday morning. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work in food service, so he doesn't know. He works at a hardware store, actually. I have terrible allergies, especially in the spring when the pollen is really bad. It coats everything in this weird yellow film. A lot of the girls at work are doing it. Sarah just got hers cut, and it looks really cute, but she has that straight hair. My hair has this natural wave to it, and if I cut it too short, it just poofs out like a triangle. I had it like that in high school, and it was a disaster. I looked like a mushroom. My mom told me it was cute, but moms have to say that, right? She used to buy me these awful floral dresses, too. Just... huge sunflowers all over them. I hate sunflowers. They're too tall, and the centers look like they're staring at you. The ones in the neighbor's yard are practically leaning over the fence right now. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my thoughts. I don't think anyone is putting them there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you—like something you see, hear, or read—are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like, things I read or see don't have secret messages for me. I read a lot of menus, obviously, working at the restaurant. We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. But this guy was squinting at the menu for like ten minutes and finally asked me what the soup of the day was because the cursive made minestrone look like a bunch of squiggles. I don't even like minestrone. Too many beans. Beans always upset my stomach, you know? I prefer a good potato soup, especially when it's cold out. It's supposed to rain tomorrow, actually. I need to remember to bring my umbrella. I always leave it in the backseat of my car. \n\nBut um... yeah, no hidden meanings or anything like that. Just regular stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you feel when you're at work and you start repeating table numbers or freezing up? Does it feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping around so much that you can't focus on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it does get really confusing. Like, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up right in the middle of service. Or I'll do things totally out of order. Like the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like that. My thoughts stay in my head. I mean, nobody can hear what I'm thinking. That would be crazy, right? If people could hear my thoughts, I'd probably get fired because I think some pretty mean things about the customers sometimes. Like this one lady who always asks for hot water with lemon instead of tea, but then she brings her own tea bags in her purse. It's so cheap. And she always leaves a terrible tip. Usually just a handful of change, like nickels and dimes. Who pays with nickels anymore? They're so heavy in your apron pocket. My apron gets so heavy by the end of the shift, it actually pulls on my neck. I need to get one of those aprons that ties around the waist instead of going over the neck. My manager says we have to wear the neck ones because it has the company logo right on the chest, but it's really bad for your posture. I read an article online about how bad posture can cause tension headaches, and I get headaches all the time. I usually just take ibuprofen, but sometimes it doesn't work. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are definitely private. No one is taking them or hearing them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts ever feel so jumbled or disconnected that it's hard for you to put them into clear sentences, or have people told you that your speech doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I speak perfectly fine. People understand me. You have to be able to talk clearly to be a waitress, you know? Communication is like, the most important part of the job. If you don't make sense, you mess up the orders. And people get really mad if you mess up their food. Especially with allergies. You have to be so specific. Gluten allergies are huge right now. It seems like everyone suddenly can't eat gluten. We have this gluten-free bun option for our burgers, but honestly, it crumbles the second you pick it up. It's basically like eating sand. I don't know why people pay two dollars extra for it. I love bread too much to ever give it up. Sourdough is my absolute favorite. I actually tried making a sourdough starter a couple of years ago, but I kept forgetting to feed it and it grew this gross, fuzzy green mold. It smelled just like old beer. My apartment kind of smells weird anyway, though, because the air vents are connected to the guy downstairs, and he cooks with so much garlic. Garlic is great, but not when you're smelling it at eight in the morning. It really gets trapped in the curtains. I bought these heavy blackout curtains because the streetlight outside my bedroom window is blinding. They're this really dark plum color... \n\nBut yeah, nobody has ever told me I don't make sense. My sentences are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being removed from your mind, like someone is taking them away, or that your thoughts are being shared out loud for others to hear, even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? No, like I said, nobody is taking my thoughts away or hearing them out loud. If someone were taking them, my head would be empty, but it's always so full. Usually it's just full of whatever music is playing on the overhead speakers at work. They play this one pop station on a loop, and the speaker right above my section is totally blown out. It makes this awful crackling noise every time there's a heavy bass line. I've asked Mr. Henderson, the owner, to fix it like a hundred times, but he never wants to spend money on anything. He won't even replace the salt shakers. The glass on half of them is chipped, and last week the lid just completely fell off one while a guy was using it, and a whole mountain of salt went right onto his fries. He was so mad. But honestly, our fries aren't even that good unless they're fresh out of the fryer. If they sit under the heat lamp for more than five minutes, they get all soggy. I like mine super crispy, and I dip them in mayonnaise instead of ketchup. I know some people think that's weird, but I heard they do it in Europe. I'd love to go to Europe someday. I actually took a French class when I was in college for a little bit, but I had to drop it because the textbook was insanely expensive. It was like two hundred dollars just for a paperback... but yeah, no, my thoughts are just my own. Nobody is taking them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, or that your thoughts are not following a clear, organized path?\n\nPatient: No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head. That sounds like something out of a sci-fi movie, you know? Like those movies where aliens take over people's brains. I watched this one movie late last night because I couldn't sleep. My upstairs neighbor got a new puppy and it was whining and scratching at the floor for hours. It sounded like a little rat trying to dig through the ceiling. I mean, I love dogs, but you have to train them. You can't just let them cry all night. I used to have a golden retriever when I was a kid, named Buster. He was the smartest dog. We trained him to fetch the newspaper from the driveway every morning. Although half the time it was completely covered in dog drool by the time my dad got it. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t connect well, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any reason?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like they jump around. I mean, maybe I talk a lot, but everything connects in my head. It's just like how conversations work, right? You start talking about one thing, and it reminds you of something else. Like, the other day at the diner, a customer asked for extra pickles, which reminded me of my aunt's homemade pickles. She used to can them every summer in these huge mason jars. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. He was always so serious, even when we were kids. He's an accountant now, which totally makes sense. He loves numbers. I hate math, honestly. Trying to figure out the tip at the end of a shift when the register acts up is the worst part of my night... but yeah, my thoughts don't just jump without a reason. My sentences are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being removed from your mind, like someone is taking them away, or that your thoughts are being shared out loud for others to hear, even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? No, like I said, nobody is taking my thoughts away or hearing them out loud. If someone were taking them, my head would be empty, but it's always so full. Usually it's just full of whatever music is playing on the overhead speakers at work. They play this one pop station on a loop, and the speaker right above my section is totally blown out. It makes this awful crackling noise every time there's a heavy bass line. I've asked Mr. Henderson, the owner, to fix it like a hundred times, but he never wants to spend money on anything. He won't even replace the salt shakers. The glass on half of them is chipped, and last week the lid just completely fell off one while a guy was using it, and a whole mountain of salt went right onto his fries. He was so mad. But honestly, our fries aren't even that good unless they're fresh out of the fryer. If they sit under the heat lamp for more than five minutes, they get all soggy. I like mine super crispy, and I dip them in mayonnaise instead of ketchup. I know some people think that's weird, but I heard they do it in Europe. I'd love to go to Europe someday. I actually took a French class when I was in college for a little bit, but I had to drop it because the textbook was insanely expensive. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... I guess my manager told me I had to come. What scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or, you know, doing things out of order. Like, I'd be standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers instead of closing checks. Table four, table seven, table nine... Nine is my lucky number, actually. I always try to park in spot number nine at my apartment complex, but this guy with a red truck keeps taking it. It's really frustrating because the red truck has this bumper sticker that says something about fishing, and I don't even like fish. It smells weird. But yeah, the restaurant... they said I was scaring the customers.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what goes through your mind when you're repeating the table numbers or thinking about the red truck? Do you feel like these thoughts are connected in a way that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Well, when I'm saying the numbers, it's just... I'm just trying to keep track, you know? Like, the numbers are important. Table four, table seven, table nine... Nine is just... it's a good number. It feels safe. But then I think about the red truck, and it's like... it's just so bright. Red is such an aggressive color. My aunt used to have a red car, actually. A little coupe. She drove it all the way to Florida once. Florida is so humid, I don't know how people live there. My hair gets so frizzy when it's humid. I tried this new conditioner, but it smells like coconut, and I really hate coconut. It reminds me of those little candies with the chocolate on the outside... what are they called? Mounds? No, Almond Joy. Almonds are okay, I guess, but they get stuck in your teeth. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? The truck? I just don't know why he has to park in spot nine. There are plenty of other spots.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or felt something that others around you don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, sometimes... like when I'm doing the closing shift, or even just at home in my own kitchen. I'll hear this woman's voice. She says my name, or she tells me I'm doing the register count wrong. It's so clear, like she's right next to me, and I'll actually answer her out loud before I realize no one else is there. \n\nMy kitchen at home has these awful yellow cabinets. I don't know who picked them out, probably the landlord. Landlords never have good taste. He came over last month to fix the sink, and he tracked mud all over my rug. It's a nice rug, too, I got it at Target on clearance. Target always has the best clearance section if you go on a Tuesday morning. I bought a really nice throw pillow there once, but my cat threw up on it. Cats are so sensitive, you know? You change their food one time, and suddenly they're sick everywhere. I used to buy the chicken flavor, but then they discontinued it, which is ridiculous because chicken is, like, the most basic flavor. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the voice... I just hear her sometimes, and I'm the only one who does.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that others say isn't real or have a hard time understanding, like a situation where you know something is happening but other people deny it?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... there's this thing at the restaurant. I keep feeling like a couple of the other servers, and even this one regular customer who always orders a Cobb salad with the dressing on the side... I think they're watching how I ring things in. Like, they're leaving these little signs on the tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work in food service, so he doesn't know. He works at a hardware store, actually. The one over on Elm Street with the big orange sign. I went in there once to buy some lightbulbs, and it's just so dusty in there. I sneezed like three times. I have terrible allergies, especially in the spring when the pollen is really bad. It coats everything in this weird yellow film. My neighbor's car was completely yellow last week, and he just bought it. It's a Honda, I think. Or maybe a Toyota? No, it's definitely a Honda because it has that little H on the back. But yeah, the tickets... they're definitely doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind that aren’t yours, or like things happening around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like thoughts are put in my head. My thoughts are my own, you know? Like, I have a lot of them, especially when it's busy at work. But they're mine. I did think about getting my hair cut short, though. Like a bob? A lot of the girls at work are doing it. Sarah just got hers cut, and it looks really cute, but she has that straight hair. My hair has this natural wave to it, and if I cut it too short, it just poofs out like a triangle. I had it like that in high school, and it was a disaster. I looked like a mushroom. My mom told me it was cute, but moms have to say that, right? She used to buy me these awful floral dresses, too. Just... huge sunflowers all over them. I hate sunflowers. They're too tall, and the centers look like they're staring at you. The ones in the neighbor's yard are practically leaning over the fence right now. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my thoughts. I don't think anyone is putting them there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you—like something you see, hear, or read—are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like, things I read or see don't have secret messages for me. I read a lot of menus, obviously, working at the restaurant. We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. But this guy was squinting at the menu for like ten minutes and finally asked me what the soup of the day was because the cursive made minestrone look like a bunch of squiggles. I don't even like minestrone. Too many beans. Beans always upset my stomach, you know? I prefer a good potato soup, especially when it's cold out. It's supposed to rain tomorrow, actually. I need to remember to bring my umbrella. I always leave it in the backseat of my car. \n\nBut um... yeah, no hidden meanings or anything like that. Just regular stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you feel when you're at work and you start repeating table numbers or freezing up? Does it feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping around so much that you can't focus on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it does get really confusing. Like, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up right in the middle of service. Or I'll do things totally out of order. Like the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like that. My thoughts stay in my head. I mean, nobody can hear what I'm thinking. That would be crazy, right? If people could hear my thoughts, I'd probably get fired because I think some pretty mean things about the customers sometimes. Like this one lady who always asks for hot water with lemon instead of tea, but then she brings her own tea bags in her purse. It's so cheap. And she always leaves a terrible tip. Usually just a handful of change, like nickels and dimes. Who pays with nickels anymore? They're so heavy in your apron pocket. My apron gets so heavy by the end of the shift, it actually pulls on my neck. I need to get one of those aprons that ties around the waist instead of going over the neck. My manager says we have to wear the neck ones because it has the company logo right on the chest, but it's really bad for your posture. I read an article online about how bad posture can cause tension headaches, and I get headaches all the time. I usually just take ibuprofen, but sometimes it doesn't work. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are definitely private. No one is taking them or hearing them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts ever feel so jumbled or disconnected that it's hard for you to put them into clear sentences, or have people told you that your speech doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I speak perfectly fine. People understand me. You have to be able to talk clearly to be a waitress, you know? Communication is like, the most important part of the job. If you don't make sense, you mess up the orders. And people get really mad if you mess up their food. Especially with allergies. You have to be so specific. Gluten allergies are huge right now. It seems like everyone suddenly can't eat gluten. We have this gluten-free bun option for our burgers, but honestly, it crumbles the second you pick it up. It's basically like eating sand. I don't know why people pay two dollars extra for it. I love bread too much to ever give it up. Sourdough is my absolute favorite. I actually tried making a sourdough starter a couple of years ago, but I kept forgetting to feed it and it grew this gross, fuzzy green mold. It smelled just like old beer. My apartment kind of smells weird anyway, though, because the air vents are connected to the guy downstairs, and he cooks with so much garlic. Garlic is great, but not when you're smelling it at eight in the morning. It really gets trapped in the curtains. I bought these heavy blackout curtains because the streetlight outside my bedroom window is blinding. They're this really dark plum color... \n\nBut yeah, nobody has ever told me I don't make sense. My sentences are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being removed from your mind, like someone is taking them away, or that your thoughts are being shared out loud for others to hear, even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? No, like I said, nobody is taking my thoughts away or hearing them out loud. If someone were taking them, my head would be empty, but it's always so full. Usually it's just full of whatever music is playing on the overhead speakers at work. They play this one pop station on a loop, and the speaker right above my section is totally blown out. It makes this awful crackling noise every time there's a heavy bass line. I've asked Mr. Henderson, the owner, to fix it like a hundred times, but he never wants to spend money on anything. He won't even replace the salt shakers. The glass on half of them is chipped, and last week the lid just completely fell off one while a guy was using it, and a whole mountain of salt went right onto his fries. He was so mad. But honestly, our fries aren't even that good unless they're fresh out of the fryer. If they sit under the heat lamp for more than five minutes, they get all soggy. I like mine super crispy, and I dip them in mayonnaise instead of ketchup. I know some people think that's weird, but I heard they do it in Europe. I'd love to go to Europe someday. I actually took a French class when I was in college for a little bit, but I had to drop it because the textbook was insanely expensive. It was like two hundred dollars just for a paperback... but yeah, no, my thoughts are just my own. Nobody is taking them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, or that your thoughts are not following a clear, organized path?\n\nPatient: No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head. That sounds like something out of a sci-fi movie, you know? Like those movies where aliens take over people's brains. I watched this one movie late last night because I couldn't sleep. My upstairs neighbor got a new puppy and it was whining and scratching at the floor for hours. It sounded like a little rat trying to dig through the ceiling. I mean, I love dogs, but you have to train them. You can't just let them cry all night. I used to have a golden retriever when I was a kid, named Buster. He was the smartest dog. We trained him to fetch the newspaper from the driveway every morning. Although half the time it was completely covered in dog drool by the time my dad got it. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t connect well, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any reason?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like they jump around. I mean, maybe I talk a lot, but everything connects in my head. It's just like how conversations work, right? You start talking about one thing, and it reminds you of something else. Like, the other day at the diner, a customer asked for extra pickles, which reminded me of my aunt's homemade pickles. She used to can them every summer in these huge mason jars. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. He was always so serious, even when we were kids. He's an accountant now, which totally makes sense. He loves numbers. I hate math, honestly. Trying to figure out the tip at the end of a shift when the register acts up is the worst part of my night... but yeah, my thoughts don't just jump without a reason. There's always a reason they go where they go.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, being taken out of your mind, or being heard by other people even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, you keep asking me this, don't you? Didn't we just talk about this? No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, and nobody can hear what I'm thinking. If people could hear what I was thinking, I probably would have been fired from the diner a long time ago. Sometimes when it's really busy and people are being rude, I think some pretty mean things. Like this one guy last Tuesday, he snapped his fingers at me to get my attention. Who does that? It's so disrespectful. I'm a waitress, not a dog. I told my manager, Mr. Henderson, about it, but he just said the customer is always right. He's always saying that. He has this little sign in his office that says it, right next to this weird bowling trophy he won in like, 1998. The gold paint on the little plastic bowler guy is all flaking off. I don't even know why he keeps it. Bowling is such a weird sport anyway, you have to wear those shoes that a thousand other people have sweat in. It's kind of gross if you think about it too much. I went bowling for a friend's birthday party a few years ago and I dropped the ball right on my toe. My toenail turned completely black and fell off a week later. It was disgusting. I had to wear closed-toe shoes all summer... but yeah, no, my thoughts are private. Just mine. Nobody else's.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t connect well, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any reason?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like they jump around. I mean, maybe I talk a lot, but everything connects in my head. It's just like how conversations work, right? You start talking about one thing, and it reminds you of something else. Like, the other day at the diner, a customer asked for extra pickles, which reminded me of my aunt's homemade pickles. She used to can them every summer in these huge mason jars. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. He was always so serious, even when we were kids. He's an accountant now, which totally makes sense. He loves numbers. I hate math, honestly. Trying to figure out the tip at the end of a shift when the register acts up is the worst part of my night... but yeah, my thoughts don't just jump without a reason. There's always a reason they go where they go.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, being taken out of your mind, or being heard by other people even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, you keep asking me this, don't you? Didn't we just talk about this? No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, and nobody can hear what I'm thinking. If people could hear what I was thinking, I probably would have been fired from the diner a long time ago. Sometimes when it's really busy and people are being rude, I think some pretty mean things. Like this one guy last Tuesday, he snapped his fingers at me to get my attention. Who does that? It's so disrespectful. I'm a waitress, not a dog. I told my manager, Mr. Henderson, about it, but he just said the customer is always right. He's always saying that. He has this little sign in his office that says it, right next to this weird bowling trophy he won in like, 1998. The gold paint on the little plastic bowler guy is all flaking off. I don't even know why he keeps it. Bowling is such a weird sport anyway, you have to wear those shoes that a thousand other people have sweat in. It's kind of gross if you think about it too much. I went bowling for a friend's birthday party a few years ago and I dropped the ball right on my toe. My toenail turned completely black and fell off a week later. It was disgusting. I had to wear closed-toe shoes all summer... but yeah, no, my thoughts are private. Just mine. Well, honestly, yes. I do feel like that sometimes at work. I keep feeling like a couple of the other servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things into the POS system. It's like they're leaving little signs on the order tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work there. He works at a hardware store. He's always coming home with sawdust on his jeans. It gets everywhere, all over the couch, the rug. We just bought this new rug, too. It's a really pretty teal color with this geometric pattern. I got it on sale at one of those big home goods stores. I love going there, you can spend hours just looking at all the throw pillows and candles. I bought this candle last week that smells like pumpkin spice, even though it's not fall yet. I just love the smell of cinnamon. It reminds me of my grandma's house. She used to make the best cinnamon rolls from scratch. She'd let the dough rise overnight, and in the morning, the whole house smelled amazing. She had this old yellow kitchen with linoleum floors that squeaked every time you walked on them. But yeah, the tickets... I just know they're sending messages through them to get me in trouble.

Didn't we just talk about this? No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, and nobody can hear what I'm thinking. If people could hear what I was thinking, I probably would have been fired from the diner a long time ago. Sometimes when it's really busy and people are being rude, I think some pretty mean things. Like this one guy last Tuesday, he snapped his fingers at me to get my attention. Who does that? It's so disrespectful. I'm a waitress, not a dog. I told my manager, Mr. Henderson, about it, but he just said the customer is always right. He's always saying that. He has this little sign in his office that says it, right next to this weird bowling trophy he won in like, 1998. The gold paint on the little plastic bowler guy is all flaking off. I don't even know why he keeps it. Bowling is such a weird sport anyway, you have to wear those shoes that a thousand other people have sweat in. It's kind of gross if you think about it too much. I went bowling for a friend's birthday party a few years ago and I dropped the ball right on my toe. My toenail turned completely black and fell off a week later. It was disgusting. I had to wear closed-toe shoes all summer... but yeah, no, my thoughts are private. Just mine. Nobody else's.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things other people are doing — like their conversations, actions, or even things on TV or social media — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, yes. I do feel like that sometimes at work. I keep feeling like a couple of the other servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things into the POS system. It's like they're leaving little signs on the order tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work there. He works at a hardware store. He's always coming home with sawdust on his jeans. It gets everywhere, all over the couch, the rug. We just bought this new rug, too. It's a really pretty teal color with this geometric pattern. I got it on sale at one of those big home goods stores. I love going there, you can spend hours just looking at all the throw pillows and candles. I bought this candle last week that smells like pumpkin spice, even though it's not fall yet. I just love the smell of cinnamon. It reminds me of my grandma's house. She used to make the best cinnamon rolls from scratch. She'd let the dough rise overnight, and in the morning, the whole house smelled amazing. She had this old yellow kitchen with linoleum floors that squeaked every time you walked on them. But yeah, the tickets... I just know they're sending messages through them to get me in trouble.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you sometimes feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t follow a clear path, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any connection?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Wait, I feel like you just asked me this a few minutes ago. Are we going in circles? But no, like I said before, my thoughts don't get mixed up or jump around for no reason. They always follow a clear path. It's just a straight line, kind of like... well, like the receipt paper on the POS machine at work. It just prints one line after another, exactly how it's supposed to. 

Except when the paper gets jammed, which happens constantly because the day shift never changes the roll right. I always have to go to the supply closet to get a new box of thermal paper. The supply closet is right next to the break room, and it always smells like burnt popcorn in there. Someone is always burning popcorn in the microwave. It's usually Dave, the line cook. I don't know how you can mess up microwave popcorn, it literally has a popcorn button right on the front of it. But he manages to scorch it every single time. It takes hours to get that smell out of your clothes. I use this lavender detergent that's supposed to get rid of tough odors, but it only does so much. I love the smell of lavender, though. I have this lavender hand lotion I keep in my apron pocket because all the handwashing at the diner dries my skin out so bad... but no, my thoughts are fine. They don't just jump around.

Nobody is taking them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, or that your thoughts are not following a clear, organized path?\n\nPatient: No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t connect well, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any reason?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like they jump around. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. Like, they're leaving these little signs on the tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work in food service, so he doesn't know. He works at a hardware store, actually. The one over on Elm Street with the big orange sign. I went in there once to buy some lightbulbs, and it's just so dusty in there. I sneezed like three times. I have terrible allergies, especially in the spring when the pollen is really bad. It coats everything in this weird yellow film. My neighbor's car was completely yellow last week, and he just bought it. It's a Honda, I think. Or maybe a Toyota? No, it's definitely a Honda because it has that little H on the back. But yeah, the tickets... they're definitely doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind that aren’t yours, or like things happening around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel like thoughts are put in my head. My thoughts are my own, you know? Like, I have a lot of them, especially when it's busy at work. But they're mine. I did think about getting my hair cut short, though. Like a bob? A lot of the girls at work are doing it. Sarah just got hers cut, and it looks really cute, but she has that straight hair. My hair has this natural wave to it, and if I cut it too short, it just poofs out like a triangle. I had it like that in high school, and it was a disaster. I looked like a mushroom. My mom told me it was cute, but moms have to say that, right? She used to buy me these awful floral dresses, too. Just... huge sunflowers all over them. I hate sunflowers. They're too tall, and the centers look like they're staring at you. The ones in the neighbor's yard are practically leaning over the fence right now. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my thoughts. I don't think anyone is putting them there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you—like something you see, hear, or read—are specifically directed at you or have a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like, things I read or see don't have secret messages for me. I read a lot of menus, obviously, working at the restaurant. We actually just updated ours last week, and they changed the font to this really loopy cursive. It is so hard to read. Older people hate it. This one older guy came in on Sunday... Sunday brunch is always the worst shift, by the way, everybody wants unlimited mimosas and they get so loud. But this guy was squinting at the menu for like ten minutes and finally asked me what the soup of the day was because the cursive made minestrone look like a bunch of squiggles. I don't even like minestrone. Too many beans. Beans always upset my stomach, you know? I prefer a good potato soup, especially when it's cold out. It's supposed to rain tomorrow, actually. I need to remember to bring my umbrella. I always leave it in the backseat of my car. \n\nBut um... yeah, no hidden meanings or anything like that. Just regular stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you feel when you're at work and you start repeating table numbers or freezing up? Does it feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping around so much that you can't focus on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it does get really confusing. Like, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up right in the middle of service. Or I'll do things totally out of order. Like the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. The soda station is always so sticky, by the way. No matter how many times we wipe it down, the syrup just gets everywhere. Especially the cherry syrup for the Shirley Temples. Kids order those all the time, and they always want extra cherries. The cherries come in these huge plastic jars, and the liquid is this unnatural bright red color. It stains your fingers if you're not careful. I got it on my favorite white shirt once, and I tried everything to get it out—bleach, baking soda, that special stain remover pen—but there's still a faint pink spot right near the collar. I can't wear it to work anymore because it doesn't look professional. We have a strict dress code. Black pants, white shirt. It's so boring. I wish we could wear colors. Maybe like a nice soft blue? Blue is my favorite color. It reminds me of the ocean. I haven't been to the beach in years... but yeah, I just freeze up and start saying the numbers instead of doing my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being taken out of your head or like your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like that. My thoughts stay in my head. I mean, nobody can hear what I'm thinking. That would be crazy, right? If people could hear my thoughts, I'd probably get fired because I think some pretty mean things about the customers sometimes. Like this one lady who always asks for hot water with lemon instead of tea, but then she brings her own tea bags in her purse. It's so cheap. And she always leaves a terrible tip. Usually just a handful of change, like nickels and dimes. Who pays with nickels anymore? They're so heavy in your apron pocket. My apron gets so heavy by the end of the shift, it actually pulls on my neck. I need to get one of those aprons that ties around the waist instead of going over the neck. My manager says we have to wear the neck ones because it has the company logo right on the chest, but it's really bad for your posture. I read an article online about how bad posture can cause tension headaches, and I get headaches all the time. I usually just take ibuprofen, but sometimes it doesn't work. \n\nBut no, my thoughts are definitely private. No one is taking them or hearing them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts ever feel so jumbled or disconnected that it's hard for you to put them into clear sentences, or have people told you that your speech doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I speak perfectly fine. People understand me. You have to be able to talk clearly to be a waitress, you know? Communication is like, the most important part of the job. If you don't make sense, you mess up the orders. And people get really mad if you mess up their food. Especially with allergies. You have to be so specific. Gluten allergies are huge right now. It seems like everyone suddenly can't eat gluten. We have this gluten-free bun option for our burgers, but honestly, it crumbles the second you pick it up. It's basically like eating sand. I don't know why people pay two dollars extra for it. I love bread too much to ever give it up. Sourdough is my absolute favorite. I actually tried making a sourdough starter a couple of years ago, but I kept forgetting to feed it and it grew this gross, fuzzy green mold. It smelled just like old beer. My apartment kind of smells weird anyway, though, because the air vents are connected to the guy downstairs, and he cooks with so much garlic. Garlic is great, but not when you're smelling it at eight in the morning. It really gets trapped in the curtains. I bought these heavy blackout curtains because the streetlight outside my bedroom window is blinding. They're this really dark plum color... \n\nBut yeah, nobody has ever told me I don't make sense. My sentences are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being removed from your mind, like someone is taking them away, or that your thoughts are being shared out loud for others to hear, even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? No, like I said, nobody is taking my thoughts away or hearing them out loud. If someone were taking them, my head would be empty, but it's always so full. Usually it's just full of whatever music is playing on the overhead speakers at work. They play this one pop station on a loop, and the speaker right above my section is totally blown out. It makes this awful crackling noise every time there's a heavy bass line. I've asked Mr. Henderson, the owner, to fix it like a hundred times, but he never wants to spend money on anything. He won't even replace the salt shakers. The glass on half of them is chipped, and last week the lid just completely fell off one while a guy was using it, and a whole mountain of salt went right onto his fries. He was so mad. But honestly, our fries aren't even that good unless they're fresh out of the fryer. If they sit under the heat lamp for more than five minutes, they get all soggy. I like mine super crispy, and I dip them in mayonnaise instead of ketchup. I know some people think that's weird, but I heard they do it in Europe. I'd love to go to Europe someday. I actually took a French class when I was in college for a little bit, but I had to drop it because the textbook was insanely expensive. It was like two hundred dollars just for a paperback... but yeah, no, my thoughts are just my own. Nobody is taking them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, or that your thoughts are not following a clear, organized path?\n\nPatient: No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head. That sounds like something out of a sci-fi movie, you know? Like those movies where aliens take over people's brains. I watched this one movie late last night because I couldn't sleep. My upstairs neighbor got a new puppy and it was whining and scratching at the floor for hours. It sounded like a little rat trying to dig through the ceiling. I mean, I love dogs, but you have to train them. You can't just let them cry all night. I used to have a golden retriever when I was a kid, named Buster. He was the smartest dog. We trained him to fetch the newspaper from the driveway every morning. Although half the time it was completely covered in dog drool by the time my dad got it. My dad used to get so annoyed, he'd have to lay the paper out on the kitchen counter to dry before he could read the sports section. He was obsessed with baseball. We went to a game once and he caught a foul ball. He kept it on a little wooden stand on his desk for years until my mom accidentally knocked it off while she was dusting and it rolled under the radiator and got all scorched. It smelled like burning rubber for a week in that house... \n\nBut no, my thoughts are just my own. They aren't put there by anybody else, and they make sense to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t connect well, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any reason?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like they jump around. I mean, maybe I talk a lot, but everything connects in my head. It's just like how conversations work, right? You start talking about one thing, and it reminds you of something else. Like, the other day at the diner, a customer asked for extra pickles, which reminded me of my aunt's homemade pickles. She used to can them every summer in these huge mason jars. She had this massive garden in her backyard, right next to this old oak tree. That tree was huge, and in the fall, it dropped acorns everywhere. We used to collect them in little buckets and pretend they were money. I tried to buy a candy bar from my brother with a handful of acorns once, but he wasn't having it. He was always so serious, even when we were kids. He's an accountant now, which totally makes sense. He loves numbers. I hate math, honestly. Trying to figure out the tip at the end of a shift when the register acts up is the worst part of my night... but yeah, my thoughts don't just jump without a reason. There's always a reason they go where they go.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, being taken out of your mind, or being heard by other people even though you're not speaking them?\n\nPatient: Wait, you keep asking me this, don't you? Didn't we just talk about this? No, nobody is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, and nobody can hear what I'm thinking. If people could hear what I was thinking, I probably would have been fired from the diner a long time ago. Sometimes when it's really busy and people are being rude, I think some pretty mean things. Like this one guy last Tuesday, he snapped his fingers at me to get my attention. Who does that? It's so disrespectful. I'm a waitress, not a dog. I told my manager, Mr. Henderson, about it, but he just said the customer is always right. He's always saying that. He has this little sign in his office that says it, right next to this weird bowling trophy he won in like, 1998. The gold paint on the little plastic bowler guy is all flaking off. I don't even know why he keeps it. Bowling is such a weird sport anyway, you have to wear those shoes that a thousand other people have sweat in. It's kind of gross if you think about it too much. I went bowling for a friend's birthday party a few years ago and I dropped the ball right on my toe. My toenail turned completely black and fell off a week later. It was disgusting. I had to wear closed-toe shoes all summer... but yeah, no, my thoughts are private. Just mine. Nobody else's.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things other people are doing — like their conversations, actions, or even things on TV or social media — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, yes. I do feel like that sometimes at work. I keep feeling like a couple of the other servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things into the POS system. It's like they're leaving little signs on the order tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband says it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work there. He works at a hardware store. He's always coming home with sawdust on his jeans. It gets everywhere, all over the couch, the rug. We just bought this new rug, too. It's a really pretty teal color with this geometric pattern. I got it on sale at one of those big home goods stores. I love going there, you can spend hours just looking at all the throw pillows and candles. I bought this candle last week that smells like pumpkin spice, even though it's not fall yet. I just love the smell of cinnamon. It reminds me of my grandma's house. She used to make the best cinnamon rolls from scratch. She'd let the dough rise overnight, and in the morning, the whole house smelled amazing. She had this old yellow kitchen with linoleum floors that squeaked every time you walked on them. But yeah, the tickets... I just know they're sending messages through them to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you sometimes feel like your thoughts are mixed up or don’t follow a clear path, like one idea suddenly jumps to another without any connection?\n\nPatient: Wait, I feel like you just asked me this a few minutes ago. Are we going in circles? But no, like I said before, my thoughts don't get mixed up or jump around for no reason. They always follow a clear path. It's just a straight line, kind of like... well, like the receipt paper on the POS machine at work. It just prints one line after another, exactly how it's supposed to. \n\nExcept when the paper gets jammed, which happens constantly because the day shift never changes the roll right. I always have to go to the supply closet to get a new box of thermal paper. The supply closet is right next to the break room, and it always smells like burnt popcorn in there. Someone is always burning popcorn in the microwave. It's usually Dave, the line cook. I don't know how you can mess up microwave popcorn, it literally has a popcorn button right on the front of it. But he manages to scorch it every single time. It takes hours to get that smell out of your clothes. I use this lavender detergent that's supposed to get rid of tough odors, but it only does so much. I love the smell of lavender, though. I have this lavender hand lotion I keep in my apron pocket because all the handwashing at the diner dries my skin out so bad... but no, my thoughts are fine. They don't just jump around.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
